Well... I suppose that's the part that's a little embarrassing, like I was trying to say when I first came in. I really do *want* to stop. I mean, my brain is right there telling me, 'Okay, Alejandro, you've had enough, time to close the fridge.' But... um... it's almost like my body just doesn't listen to me? 

I just keep reaching for another bite, even when I know I shouldn't. It's probably just a silly lack of willpower on my part, really, nothing too dramatic. But to answer your question... no, once I get going, I don't really feel like I can just stop myself. 

I hope that's helpful? I know it sounds a bit foolish for a man my age to say he can't stop snacking, but I want to be completely honest with you so you have all the right information.

Oh, well... I suppose it's a little embarrassing to admit, but I definitely do think about stopping. It's just that once I start picking at the leftovers, it feels like the sensible part of me is right there saying, 'That’s enough, Alejandro, you don't need any more.' I wouldn't call it some big, dramatic compulsion, though. It's really just a... a little lapse in willpower in the moment, I think. I hope that doesn't make me sound foolish? I mean, it's fairly normal to have a hard time resisting a tasty dish once it's right in front of you, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm answering your question the right way and giving you what you need.

Oh, well... I suppose, if I'm being completely open with you... yes, it does sometimes end up being a bit more than I originally planned. Not an enormous amount, of course! But... well, some nights after supper, when I’m supposed to be just packing up the food—like the leftover tamales or the mole—I’ll somehow end up eating several extra servings right there. 

And then, well, maybe I'll take some pan dulce out to the garage with me to finish off. If I'm being perfectly honest, it’s definitely more food than I’d ever put on a plate if my cousin or the family were in the kitchen watching me.

Well, I suppose... it kind of goes back to what I was mentioning a little earlier. It's a bit tough to admit out loud, but... no, I don't always feel like I'm the one steering the ship right then.

feels like the sensible part of me is saying, 'That’s enough, Alejandro,' but my hand keeps going back for more anyway.\n- key: S052\n  name: Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full\n  description: Eating description: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n  manifestation: I make sure I do it after the house is quiet or out in the garage because I’d be embarrassed for ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, upset is... it's a strong word, isn't it? I wouldn't say I'm terribly upset, no. I mean, it's just a little habit, really. 

But... I suppose, if I'm being perfectly honest with you... it does bother me a little bit. Especially the next morning. I wake up and I think about the pan dulce and the extra tamales, and I just feel... well, a little disappointed in myself. I know I should be doing better at my age. It's a bit embarrassing, to be frank. 

I try not to let it get me down too much, though! I just try to focus on having a better day. I don't want you to think I'm sitting around crying over spilled milk, or anything like that. I just... I wish I didn't do it, that's all. 

Does that make sense? I hope I'm not making too big a deal out of it, but I want to be sure I'm giving you the right answer.

Well, guilty... I suppose that's similar to what we were just talking about, isn't it? I wouldn't say I feel a deep, terrible guilt, like I've committed a crime or anything. It's just food, after all. 

But, yes, I do feel a little... I guess you could call it a twinge of guilt, sometimes. Just because I know I shouldn't be eating that much right before bed, especially the sweets. It's more of a 'why did I do that again' kind of feeling, rather than a heavy burden of guilt. 

I don't dwell on it, though! I promise I'm not beating myself up over it all day. I just want to be completely transparent with you so you can do your job. Is that the sort of feeling you were asking about?

I just want to make sure I'm explaining things properly for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel stressed, anxious, or upset before you start picking at the leftovers late at night?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I wouldn't really say so. I mean, my life is pretty quiet these days, so there isn't much to be stressed about, thankfully. I don't really feel... upset or anxious, exactly, before it happens. \n\nIt's more just... well, the food is there in the fridge, and I just sort of start without much of a reason. I haven't really noticed any big emotional thing setting it off, to be perfectly honest. \n\nI hope that's the right kind of answer? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to figure this out. Is it okay if I don't have a specific reason like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can stop yourself from picking at the leftovers if you want to?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose that's the part that's a little embarrassing, like I was trying to say when I first came in. I really do *want* to stop. I mean, my brain is right there telling me, 'Okay, Alejandro, you've had enough, time to close the fridge.' But... um... it's almost like my body just doesn't listen to me? \n\nI just keep reaching for another bite, even when I know I shouldn't. It's probably just a silly lack of willpower on my part, really, nothing too dramatic. But to answer your question... no, once I get going, I don't really feel like I can just stop myself. \n\nI hope that's helpful? I know it sounds a bit foolish for a man my age to say he can't stop snacking, but I want to be completely honest with you so you have all the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself losing interest in activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I still enjoy my usual routine. I like to tend to the garden, read the paper, maybe watch a bit of television in the evening... all the normal things I've always liked doing. \n\nI wouldn't say I've lost interest in any of that. I'm still quite content with my day-to-day life, really. I hope that's a good sign? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of things. I'm not... well, I'm not depressed or anything like that, if that's what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after eating the leftovers late at night?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I wouldn't say I feel ashamed. That sounds a bit... well, a bit heavy, you know? It's really just food, after all. \n\nI might wake up the next morning and think, 'Alejandro, you didn't really need to finish off that extra piece of chicken,' but it doesn't weigh on my conscience or anything like that. It's more just a... a silly little habit I'd like to tidy up, rather than something I beat myself up over. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I know some people get very upset about their eating, but I just try not to make a big fuss over it. I hope it's alright that I don't feel too badly about it? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right idea of how it affects me.\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself picking at leftovers late at night?\n\nPatient: Well, let's see... I suppose if I really think about it, it's been happening maybe once or twice a week? Most weeks, anyway. \n\nIt actually started around the time my cousin moved in with me. We've been having some lovely dinners together, which is nice, but it just means there's a bit more food around the house than I'm used to. So, yes, probably one or two nights a week I'll find myself having a little extra. \n\nI hope that's not too terrible? I mean, a couple of nights a week doesn't sound too out of the ordinary, does it? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right timeline so you can understand the situation properly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating the leftovers because you can't resist them, or is it more because you just don't think about stopping?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose it's a little embarrassing to admit, but I definitely do think about stopping. It's just that once I start picking at the leftovers, it feels like the sensible part of me is right there saying, 'That’s enough, Alejandro, you don't need any more.' But... well, my hand just sort of keeps going back for another bite anyway. \n\nI wouldn't call it some big, dramatic compulsion, though. It's really just a... a little lapse in willpower in the moment, I think. I hope that doesn't make me sound foolish? I mean, it's fairly normal to have a hard time resisting a tasty dish once it's right in front of you, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm answering your question the right way and giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Has picking at leftovers late at night interfered with your daily routine or activities in any way?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not. I wouldn't let a little habit like that get in the way of my actual life. I’m still... well, I'm still keeping up with all my usual errands, my appointments, and making sure I help out around the house. I like to stay busy and pull my weight, you know? \n\nI suppose... if I’m being completely honest... the mornings after it happens, I might feel just a tiny bit sluggish. And maybe... well, maybe just a little less proud of how I’m handling myself in that regard. But it really doesn't stop me from getting things done! I just shake it off and get on with my day. \n\nI hope that's alright? I just want to make sure you know I'm still functioning perfectly well, but I also want to give you the honest picture. Is that the sort of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating more than you intended to when you start picking at the leftovers?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, well, yes, absolutely. I eat plenty during the day. My wife makes sure of that! We always sit down and have a lovely, filling dinner together. So, it's not that I'm starving or anything like that. 

To be perfectly honest... and I hope this doesn't sound too foolish... a lot of the time when I'm up late, I'm not even hungry at all. I mean, my stomach isn't rumbling. It's just... I'll wander into the kitchen, and before I know it, I'm opening up the containers and just having a little taste of this, and a little taste of that. 

It's silly, I know, since I've already had a good meal. I suppose I just enjoy the flavors? I don't want you to think I'm completely out of control or anything, it's just a bit of mindless grazing. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right for you.

That sounds a bit... well, a bit heavy, you know? It's really just food, after all. \n\nI might wake up the next morning and think, 'Alejandro, you didn't really need to finish off that extra piece of chicken,' but it doesn't weigh on my conscience or anything like that. It's more just a... a silly little habit I'd like to tidy up, rather than something I beat myself up over. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I know some people get very upset about their eating, but I just try not to make a big fuss over it. I hope it's alright that I don't feel too badly about it? We've been having some lovely dinners together, which is nice, but it just means there's a bit more food around the house than I'm used to. So, yes, probably one or two nights a week I'll find myself having a little extra. \n\nI hope that's not too terrible? I mean, a couple of nights a week doesn't sound too out of the ordinary, does it? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right timeline so you can understand the situation properly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating the leftovers because you can't resist them, or is it more because you just don't think about stopping?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose it's a little embarrassing to admit, but I definitely do think about stopping. But... well, my hand just sort of keeps going back for another bite anyway. \n\nI wouldn't call it some big, dramatic compulsion, though. It's really just a... a little lapse in willpower in the moment, I think. I hope that doesn't make me sound foolish? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh... well, yes. Yes, absolutely. If someone walked in, I would stop right away. In fact... well, to be completely honest with you—and I hope you won't think too poorly of me—I actually make sure I only do it after my wife has gone to bed and the house is completely quiet. Or sometimes I'll even take a little something out to the garage. 

I just... I would be terribly embarrassed if my family, or my cousin who visits, saw how much I was actually eating during those late-night trips. I've always been sort of looked at as the disciplined one in the family, you know? The one who has his act together. So, letting them see me picking away at all those containers... well, it would just be a bit shameful. 

So yes, I'd definitely stop if someone was watching. I hope that doesn't make me sound terribly deceptive? I just prefer to keep this silly little habit to myself so I don't worry anyone. Does that make sense?

It's more just a... a silly little habit I'd like to tidy up, rather than something I beat myself up over. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I know some people get very upset about their eating, but I just try not to make a big fuss over it. I hope it's alright that I don't feel too badly about it? I suppose if I really think about it, it's been happening maybe once or twice a week? Most weeks, anyway. \n\nIt actually started around the time my cousin moved in with me. I suppose it's a little embarrassing to admit, but I definitely do think about stopping. It's just that once I start picking at the leftovers, it feels like the sensible part of me is right there saying, 'That’s enough, Alejandro, you don't need any more.' But... well, my hand just sort of keeps going back for another bite anyway. \n\nI wouldn't call it some big, dramatic compulsion, though. It's really just a... a little lapse in willpower in the moment, I think. I hope that doesn't make me sound foolish? I mean, it's fairly normal to have a hard time resisting a tasty dish once it's right in front of you, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm answering your question the right way and giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Has picking at leftovers late at night interfered with your daily routine or activities in any way?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not. I wouldn't let a little habit like that get in the way of my actual life. I’m still... well, I'm still keeping up with all my usual errands, my appointments, and making sure I help out around the house. I like to stay busy and pull my weight, you know? \n\nI suppose... if I’m being completely honest... the mornings after it happens, I might feel just a tiny bit sluggish. And maybe... well, maybe just a little less proud of how I’m handling myself in that regard. But it really doesn't stop me from getting things done! I just shake it off and get on with my day. \n\nI hope that's alright? I just want to make sure you know I'm still functioning perfectly well, but I also want to give you the honest picture. I suppose, if I'm being completely open with you... yes, it does sometimes end up being a bit more than I originally planned. Not an enormous amount, of course! But... well, some nights after supper, when I’m supposed to be just packing up the food—like the leftover tamales or the mole—I’ll somehow end up eating several extra servings right there. \n\nAnd then, well, maybe I'll take some pan dulce out to the garage with me to finish off. If I'm being perfectly honest, it’s definitely more food than I’d ever put on a plate if my cousin or the family were in the kitchen watching me. \n\nI know it sounds a bit silly, a man my age hiding sweet bread in the garage. I hope you don't judge me too harshly for that? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the exact details you need so you can understand the whole picture. Am I answering that okay for you?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of your eating when you're picking at the leftovers late at night?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose... it kind of goes back to what I was mentioning a little earlier. It's a bit tough to admit out loud, but... no, I don't always feel like I'm the one steering the ship right then. \n\nIt's not like I completely lose my mind, of course! I'm still there. But... well, like I said before, my brain is right there telling me to stop, saying, 'Alejandro, you've had enough, put it away.' And yet, my hand just doesn't seem to listen. It just keeps going back for more, even when I really, truly want to stop. \n\nIt's just a temporary little lapse in the moment, though. I hope that makes sense? I really want to make sure I'm explaining it clearly for you, without sounding too weak-willed. Is that what you meant by feeling in control?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset or bothered by your late-night eating habits?\n\nPatient: Well, upset is... it's a strong word, isn't it? I wouldn't say I'm terribly upset, no. I mean, it's just a little habit, really. \n\nBut... I suppose, if I'm being perfectly honest with you... it does bother me a little bit. Especially the next morning. I wake up and I think about the pan dulce and the extra tamales, and I just feel... well, a little disappointed in myself. I know I should be doing better at my age. It's a bit embarrassing, to be frank. \n\nI try not to let it get me down too much, though! I just try to focus on having a better day. I don't want you to think I'm sitting around crying over spilled milk, or anything like that. I just... I wish I didn't do it, that's all. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm not making too big a deal out of it, but I want to be sure I'm giving you the right answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel guilty after eating the leftovers late at night?\n\nPatient: Well, guilty... I suppose that's similar to what we were just talking about, isn't it? I wouldn't say I feel a deep, terrible guilt, like I've committed a crime or anything. Am I answering that okay for you?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of your eating when you're picking at the leftovers late at night?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose... it kind of goes back to what I was mentioning a little earlier. It just keeps going back for more, even when I really, truly want to stop. \n\nIt's just a temporary little lapse in the moment, though. I hope that makes sense? Maybe I'm just a little bored sometimes if I'm up late and the television isn't very interesting? Oh, well... I suppose it's a bit of both, but... well, if I'm being perfectly honest, it's more about the stopping. 

It's actually the main reason I decided to come see you. It's just... well, once I start picking at the leftovers, it's like the sensible part of my brain is right there, saying, 'That's enough, Alejandro, you don't need any more.' But... my hand just sort of keeps going back for another bite anyway. 

It's so silly, isn't it? A grown man who can't just close a Tupperware container. I really try to just have a little taste, but it's like I just get a bit carried away once I've opened the fridge. I hope that doesn't make me sound completely out of control or anything like that? It's really just a minor struggle with willpower in the moment, I think. Is that the sort of thing you were asking about?

I'm not... well, I'm not depressed or anything like that, if that's what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after eating the leftovers late at night?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I wouldn't say I feel ashamed. That sounds a bit... well, a bit heavy, you know? It's really just food, after all. \n\nI might wake up the next morning and think, 'Alejandro, you didn't really need to finish off that extra piece of chicken,' but it doesn't weigh on my conscience or anything like that. It's more just a... a silly little habit I'd like to tidy up, rather than something I beat myself up over. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I know some people get very upset about their eating, but I just try not to make a big fuss over it. I hope it's alright that I don't feel too badly about it? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right idea of how it affects me.\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself picking at leftovers late at night?\n\nPatient: Well, let's see... I suppose if I really think about it, it's been happening maybe once or twice a week? Most weeks, anyway. \n\nIt actually started around the time my cousin moved in with me. We've been having some lovely dinners together, which is nice, but it just means there's a bit more food around the house than I'm used to. So, yes, probably one or two nights a week I'll find myself having a little extra. \n\nI hope that's not too terrible? I mean, a couple of nights a week doesn't sound too out of the ordinary, does it? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right timeline so you can understand the situation properly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating the leftovers because you can't resist them, or is it more because you just don't think about stopping?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose it's a little embarrassing to admit, but I definitely do think about stopping. It's just that once I start picking at the leftovers, it feels like the sensible part of me is right there saying, 'That’s enough, Alejandro, you don't need any more.' But... well, my hand just sort of keeps going back for another bite anyway. \n\nI wouldn't call it some big, dramatic compulsion, though. It's really just a... a little lapse in willpower in the moment, I think. I hope that doesn't make me sound foolish? I mean, it's fairly normal to have a hard time resisting a tasty dish once it's right in front of you, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm answering your question the right way and giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Has picking at leftovers late at night interfered with your daily routine or activities in any way?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not. I wouldn't let a little habit like that get in the way of my actual life. Oh, goodness... just one single bite? Hmm. I... I don't know that I've ever really tested myself quite like that, to be perfectly honest.

I don't really feel... upset or anxious, exactly, before it happens. \n\nIt's more just... well, the food is there in the fridge, and I just sort of start without much of a reason. I haven't really noticed any big emotional thing setting it off, to be perfectly honest. \n\nI hope that's the right kind of answer? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to figure this out. Is it okay if I don't have a specific reason like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can stop yourself from picking at the leftovers if you want to?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose that's the part that's a little embarrassing, like I was trying to say when I first came in. I really do *want* to stop. I mean, my brain is right there telling me, 'Okay, Alejandro, you've had enough, time to close the fridge.' But... um... it's almost like my body just doesn't listen to me? \n\nI just keep reaching for another bite, even when I know I shouldn't. It's probably just a silly lack of willpower on my part, really, nothing too dramatic. But to answer your question... no, once I get going, I don't really feel like I can just stop myself. \n\nI hope that's helpful? I know it sounds a bit foolish for a man my age to say he can't stop snacking, but I want to be completely honest with you so you have all the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself losing interest in activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I still enjoy my usual routine. I like to tend to the garden, read the paper, maybe watch a bit of television in the evening... all the normal things I've always liked doing. \n\nI wouldn't say I've lost interest in any of that. I'm still quite content with my day-to-day life, really. I hope that's a good sign? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of things. I'm not... well, I'm not depressed or anything like that, if that's what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after eating the leftovers late at night?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I wouldn't say I feel ashamed. That sounds a bit... well, a bit heavy, you know? It's really just food, after all. \n\nI might wake up the next morning and think, 'Alejandro, you didn't really need to finish off that extra piece of chicken,' but it doesn't weigh on my conscience or anything like that. It's more just a... a silly little habit I'd like to tidy up, rather than something I beat myself up over. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I know some people get very upset about their eating, but I just try not to make a big fuss over it. I hope it's alright that I don't feel too badly about it? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right idea of how it affects me.\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself picking at leftovers late at night?\n\nPatient: Well, let's see... I suppose if I really think about it, it's been happening maybe once or twice a week? Most weeks, anyway. \n\nIt actually started around the time my cousin moved in with me. We've been having some lovely dinners together, which is nice, but it just means there's a bit more food around the house than I'm used to. So, yes, probably one or two nights a week I'll find myself having a little extra. \n\nI hope that's not too terrible? I mean, a couple of nights a week doesn't sound too out of the ordinary, does it? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right timeline so you can understand the situation properly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating the leftovers because you can't resist them, or is it more because you just don't think about stopping?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose it's a little embarrassing to admit, but I definitely do think about stopping. It's just that once I start picking at the leftovers, it feels like the sensible part of me is right there saying, 'That’s enough, Alejandro, you don't need any more.' But... well, my hand just sort of keeps going back for another bite anyway. \n\nI wouldn't call it some big, dramatic compulsion, though. It's really just a... a little lapse in willpower in the moment, I think. I hope that doesn't make me sound foolish? I mean, it's fairly normal to have a hard time resisting a tasty dish once it's right in front of you, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm answering your question the right way and giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Has picking at leftovers late at night interfered with your daily routine or activities in any way?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not. I wouldn't let a little habit like that get in the way of my actual life. I’m still... well, I'm still keeping up with all my usual errands, my appointments, and making sure I help out around the house. I like to stay busy and pull my weight, you know? \n\nI suppose... if I’m being completely honest... the mornings after it happens, I might feel just a tiny bit sluggish. And maybe... well, maybe just a little less proud of how I’m handling myself in that regard. But it really doesn't stop me from getting things done! I just shake it off and get on with my day. \n\nI hope that's alright? I just want to make sure you know I'm still functioning perfectly well, but I also want to give you the honest picture. Is that the sort of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating more than you intended to when you start picking at the leftovers?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose, if I'm being completely open with you... yes, it does sometimes end up being a bit more than I originally planned. Not an enormous amount, of course! But... well, some nights after supper, when I’m supposed to be just packing up the food—like the leftover tamales or the mole—I’ll somehow end up eating several extra servings right there. \n\nAnd then, well, maybe I'll take some pan dulce out to the garage with me to finish off. If I'm being perfectly honest, it’s definitely more food than I’d ever put on a plate if my cousin or the family were in the kitchen watching me. \n\nI know it sounds a bit silly, a man my age hiding sweet bread in the garage. I hope you don't judge me too harshly for that? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the exact details you need so you can understand the whole picture. Am I answering that okay for you?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of your eating when you're picking at the leftovers late at night?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose... it kind of goes back to what I was mentioning a little earlier. It's a bit tough to admit out loud, but... no, I don't always feel like I'm the one steering the ship right then. \n\nIt's not like I completely lose my mind, of course! I'm still there. But... well, like I said before, my brain is right there telling me to stop, saying, 'Alejandro, you've had enough, put it away.' And yet, my hand just doesn't seem to listen. It just keeps going back for more, even when I really, truly want to stop. \n\nIt's just a temporary little lapse in the moment, though. I hope that makes sense? I really want to make sure I'm explaining it clearly for you, without sounding too weak-willed. Is that what you meant by feeling in control?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset or bothered by your late-night eating habits?\n\nPatient: Well, upset is... it's a strong word, isn't it? I wouldn't say I'm terribly upset, no. I mean, it's just a little habit, really. \n\nBut... I suppose, if I'm being perfectly honest with you... it does bother me a little bit. Especially the next morning. I wake up and I think about the pan dulce and the extra tamales, and I just feel... well, a little disappointed in myself. I know I should be doing better at my age. It's a bit embarrassing, to be frank. \n\nI try not to let it get me down too much, though! I just try to focus on having a better day. I don't want you to think I'm sitting around crying over spilled milk, or anything like that. I just... I wish I didn't do it, that's all. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm not making too big a deal out of it, but I want to be sure I'm giving you the right answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel guilty after eating the leftovers late at night?\n\nPatient: Well, guilty... I suppose that's similar to what we were just talking about, isn't it? I wouldn't say I feel a deep, terrible guilt, like I've committed a crime or anything. It's just food, after all. \n\nBut, yes, I do feel a little... I guess you could call it a twinge of guilt, sometimes. Just because I know I shouldn't be eating that much right before bed, especially the sweets. It's more of a 'why did I do that again' kind of feeling, rather than a heavy burden of guilt. \n\nI don't dwell on it, though! I promise I'm not beating myself up over it all day. I just want to be completely transparent with you so you can do your job. Is that the sort of feeling you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you might be eating the leftovers late at night because you're trying to cope with any emotions or feelings?\n\nPatient: Emotions? Oh, um... well, no, I really don't think so. I mean, I'm a pretty content fellow overall! I don't really have a lot of stress or anything these days, being retired and all. \n\nI think it's mostly just... well, the food is sitting right there in the kitchen, and it just tastes too good to ignore. Maybe I'm just a little bored sometimes if I'm up late and the television isn't very interesting? But I'm certainly not eating because I'm sad or upset, or trying to cope with anything heavy like that. \n\nI hope that's an okay answer? I know you're trying to get a complete picture, and I want to be as helpful to you as I can... I just don't think my feelings have much to do with it. Does that help answer your question?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're getting enough to eat during the day, before you start picking at leftovers at night?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, yes, absolutely. I eat plenty during the day. My wife makes sure of that! We always sit down and have a lovely, filling dinner together. So, it's not that I'm starving or anything like that. \n\nTo be perfectly honest... and I hope this doesn't sound too foolish... a lot of the time when I'm up late, I'm not even hungry at all. I mean, my stomach isn't rumbling. It's just... I'll wander into the kitchen, and before I know it, I'm opening up the containers and just having a little taste of this, and a little taste of that. \n\nIt's silly, I know, since I've already had a good meal. I suppose I just enjoy the flavors? I don't want you to think I'm completely out of control or anything, it's just a bit of mindless grazing. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel hungry when you start picking at the leftovers late at night?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I think I actually just touched on that a moment ago, didn't I? But, yes, to answer your question directly... no, I'm usually not hungry at all. \n\nLike I was saying, my wife and I always have a wonderful, hearty dinner together. So my stomach is perfectly full. But then later on, I just... I find myself back in the kitchen, opening up the containers and just tasting one thing after another. \n\nI really hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is! It's just a little habit I've picked up, I suppose. It's not like I'm ravenous or anything like that. Does that help clarify things for you? I want to make sure I'm giving you exactly what you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's something missing in your life that you're trying to fill with food late at night?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, I really don't think so. I mean, my life is wonderful, it really is. I've got my lovely wife, a nice home, and I'm enjoying my retirement... I don't feel like I'm missing out on anything at all. \n\nI know sometimes people eat because they're lonely or unfulfilled, but that's honestly not me. I'm very happy with how things are. I think it really is just... well, just a silly little habit of wandering into the kitchen when the house is quiet. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound too oblivious or anything? I'm trying to be as thoughtful about your questions as I can, I just... I really don't feel like there's a void I'm trying to fill. I hope that's the kind of information you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be able to stop eating the leftovers if someone was watching you?\n\nPatient: Oh... well, yes. Yes, absolutely. If someone walked in, I would stop right away. In fact... well, to be completely honest with you—and I hope you won't think too poorly of me—I actually make sure I only do it after my wife has gone to bed and the house is completely quiet. Or sometimes I'll even take a little something out to the garage. \n\nI just... I would be terribly embarrassed if my family, or my cousin who visits, saw how much I was actually eating during those late-night trips. I've always been sort of looked at as the disciplined one in the family, you know? The one who has his act together. So, letting them see me picking away at all those containers... well, it would just be a bit shameful. \n\nSo yes, I'd definitely stop if someone was watching. I hope that doesn't make me sound terribly deceptive? I just prefer to keep this silly little habit to myself so I don't worry anyone. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your late-night eating is something you've just gotten used to doing, or is it something that happens when you're not paying attention to your actions?\n\nPatient: Oh, hmm... that's a very good question, doctor. I'm... I'm actually not entirely sure, to be perfectly honest. I haven't really thought about it quite like that before. \n\nI suppose... well, maybe it's just something I've gotten used to doing? Like a little routine. I don't think I'm completely checking out or, you know, not paying attention to what I'm doing. I know I'm standing there in the kitchen. It just feels like a... well, a harmless little evening ritual, really. \n\nI'm sorry I don't have a more exact answer for you on that one. I just haven't analyzed it too deeply, since it's always felt like such a minor thing. Does that make sense, though? I hope I'm still giving you the kind of information you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your late-night eating is something you do to comfort yourself or is it more about not being able to stop once you start?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose it's a bit of both, but... well, if I'm being perfectly honest, it's more about the stopping. \n\nIt's actually the main reason I decided to come see you. It's just... well, once I start picking at the leftovers, it's like the sensible part of my brain is right there, saying, 'That's enough, Alejandro, you don't need any more.' But... my hand just sort of keeps going back for another bite anyway. \n\nIt's so silly, isn't it? A grown man who can't just close a Tupperware container. I really try to just have a little taste, but it's like I just get a bit carried away once I've opened the fridge. I hope that doesn't make me sound completely out of control or anything like that? It's really just a minor struggle with willpower in the moment, I think. Is that the sort of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be unable to stop eating the leftovers even if you tried to stop after just one bite?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... just one single bite? Hmm. I... I don't know that I've ever really tested myself quite like that, to be perfectly honest. \n\nUsually, when I go to the fridge, I'm intending to have, well, a small scoop or a little bowl, you know? I mean, I'm sure I could, right? I don't feel like I'm missing out on anything at all. \n\nI know sometimes people eat because they're lonely or unfulfilled, but that's honestly not me. I'm very happy with how things are. I think it really is just... well, just a silly little habit of wandering into the kitchen when the house is quiet. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound too oblivious or anything? I'm trying to be as thoughtful about your questions as I can, I just... I really don't feel like there's a void I'm trying to fill. Or sometimes I'll even take a little something out to the garage. \n\nI just... I would be terribly embarrassed if my family, or my cousin who visits, saw how much I was actually eating during those late-night trips. I've always been sort of looked at as the disciplined one in the family, you know? The one who has his act together. So, letting them see me picking away at all those containers... well, it would just be a bit shameful. \n\nSo yes, I'd definitely stop if someone was watching. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your late-night eating is something you've just gotten used to doing, or is it something that happens when you're not paying attention to your actions?\n\nPatient: Oh, hmm... that's a very good question, doctor. I just haven't analyzed it too deeply, since it's always felt like such a minor thing. Does that make sense, though?
